I also checked the escape hatch: the CC-BY-4.0 OpenSky Zenodo release (record 5815448) is FLIGHT LISTS only (flightlist_YYYYMMDD csv.gz — no lat/lon time series), and is frozen "no longer updated after January 2022"; the maintained successor (record 7923702, 2023) is licensed 'other-nc', i.e. OpenSky deliberately moved to non-commercial. HOWEVER, a genuine rescue exists and I verified it end to end: adsb.lol globe_history (https://github.com/adsblol/globe_history_2023) states in its README "This database is made available under the Open Database License: http://opendatacommons.org/licenses/odbl/1.0/", 1077 GiB for 2023, ~1.3-1.8 GB per day, one GitHub release per day, global coverage, pooled from adsb.lol + FlyItalyADSB + TheAirTraffic. I streamed 1.6 GB of the 2023-09-01 release and extracted 44,742 real traces in ~45 seconds through this container's proxy. ODbL is redistributable with share-alike (derived databases must also be ODbL — friction for a commercial buyer, fine for an open artifact). FAA CIFP is US-government public domain, downloaded from https://aeronav.faa.gov/Upload_313-d/cifp/CIFP_260806.zip (9.1 MB zip, 53 MB FAACIFP18, ARINC 424-18); faa.gov robots.txt does not disallow it. So: the corpus named in the pitch fails outright, but the gate is repairable by swapping OpenSky for adsb.lol. G1 is not what kills this candidate — I mark it MARGINAL because the proposal as written is unusable and would have to be rebuilt on a different corpus.

**G4 — re-check**

Crowded, and worse than round 1's 6/10. Round 1 already had ATSCC/ATFMTraj (KAIST 2024), Zhang et al. (IEEE DASC 2022), Jung/Hardy/Kochenderfer (AIAA JAIS 2025) plus three more. Via Crossref and EuropePMC (arXiv and Semantic Scholar were dead ends here — arXiv's API returns zero entries for quoted-AND queries and S2 rate-limited at 429; ATM research does not live on arXiv anyway) I added: "Standard Procedure-Guided Flight Trajectory Pattern Mining for Airport Terminal Airspace", Int. J. Aeronautical & Space Sciences 2024, doi 10.1007/s42405-024-00732-6 — this is procedure-guided matching of terminal trajectories, i.e. essentially the task; "Hierarchical Method for Mining a Prevailing Flight Pattern in Airport Terminal Airspace", AIAA JAIS 2023, doi 10.2514/1.i011263; "Improving Efficiency Through the Publication of Expected Distances for Standard Terminal Arrival Routes", Journal of Open Aviation Science 2025, doi 10.59490/joas.2024.7894 (open-data STAR-conformance work, with a published peer review at doi 10.59490/joas.2024.8041); "Hierarchical Behaviors for Characterizing Trajectory Patterns in Terminal Airspace", AIAA SciTech 2024, doi 10.2514/6.2024-2354; "ADS-B-based Characterization of Flight Behaviors in Terminal Airspace for Safety Applications", AIAA Aviation 2025; "Characterizing Terminal Airspace Operational States and Detecting Airspace-Level Anomalies", 9th OpenSky Symposium 2021 (the single most domain-specific venue); "Research on Coding Of Conventional Procedures Based Upon ARINC Specification 424", IEEE 2019. On the open-dataset half of the gate: "Image, speech, and ADS-B trajectory datasets for terminal airspace operations", Scientific Data 2025, doi 10.1038/s41597-025-04775-6 — an open terminal-airspace ADS-B dataset already exists (I did NOT verify its scale; COULD NOT VERIFY whether it reaches 10^6). Plus the tooling: xoolive/traffic already implements the geometric primitives, and Zenodo carries CC-BY terminal-area trajectory sets (record 10210178, landings at 20 large European airports 2019-2023; record 13896127, all Nantes arrivals/departures 2020-2023). That is well over 3 serious efforts, in two domain-specific venues (OpenSky Symposium, AIAA Aviation/SciTech/JAIS), plus an existing open dataset and an existing open toolchain.

**G6 — feasibility**

G6 is the one gate this passes comfortably, which is why it is worth stating plainly that G6 is not the problem. Measured: median real terminal arrival = 184 points (p90 286), so 4.3 KB/unit as raw float32 x 6 channels at native sample rate, or 3.0 KB/unit downsampled to a fixed 128 x 6 float32 tensor -> 3.07 GB for 10^6 units, comfortably inside 23 GB free disk and streamable in shards under 15 GB RAM. Yield: 1.6 GB of one day's adsb.lol tar gave 3,720 US arrivals at 14 airports; extrapolating across all 3,070 CIFP procedure airports, roughly 3-4 x 10^4 US terminal arrivals per day, so ~30 days of the 365-day 2023 archive reaches 10^6 — about 45 GB to download, which needs staged streaming but is workable. Model: a 1-D CNN or small GRU over 128 timesteps x 6 channels with an airport-conditioned softmax over <=20 classes is 1-5 M parameters; that is seconds per epoch at the 1k rung and low single-digit CPU-hours at the 1M rung on 4 cores. Four rungs at 1k/10k/100k/1M in well under a day of compute, with data acquisition the dominant cost. Everything here works. The problem is that the curve it would draw is over an ~8-point residual of a rule that needs no training.

**Strongest case against**

My matcher is crude, and I want that on the record. It labels only 38.3% overall and produces obviously broken per-airport rates at KJFK (0%) and KSFO (2%), where real arrivals are almost universally vectored and where my simple concatenation of CIFP transitions plainly under-reconstructs the published routes. A careful manufacturer — using runway configuration, altitude/speed constraints, time-windowed segment matching, and proper ARINC path-terminator expansion for RF/CF/VA legs — would label substantially more than 38.3%. Someone defending this candidate would say my 87% dumb-prefix number is measured on an unrepresentative easy subset. Two answers. First, the 87% is a within-matcher comparison — the same code, same routes, same fit threshold, full trajectory versus 40% prefix — so it is robust to how good the matcher is in absolute terms. Second and decisively, the defence points the wrong way: a BETTER manufacturer labels MORE flights for free and therefore SHRINKS the residual the learned model is supposed to own. Improving the teacher makes the student less necessary. The candidate is squeezed from both sides and there is no matcher quality at which it escapes. The other case against me is G1: I found a real fix (adsb.lol, ODbL) that the pitch missed, so a reviewer could say I should have credited the candidate for a repairable corpus. I did credit it — G1 is marked MARGINAL, not FAIL. It simply is not what kills this. I also could not verify my suspicion that commercial airport noise-monitoring systems (ANOMS, Casper, Topsonic and similar) already ship flight-track-to-published-route correlation as a standard feature, which if true would further erode G5; I did not test it and record it as COULD NOT VERIFY.

**Method.** *attack_round1_leftovers*: 2 agents adversarially reviewing the two round-1 survivors that a mechanical top-4 slice had cut before review → *g4_then_adversary*: pipeline: white-space verification per survivor, then adversarial review for any survivor whose G4 verdict was not FAIL → *generation*: 3 scouts organised by ASYMMETRY STRUCTURE rather than sector: (1) the future is the label, (2) a richer instrument is the label, (3) the generator still exists - delete it and predict it → *screen*: 1 screener cutting on G2a first, then A2, then A1, then G1/G6/G3/G5

## Round 2: 4 survivors carried to white-space verification

### 1. AllocTrace - recovering the census imputation mask from released microdata

Given a US Census ACS PUMS person record with its allocation flags deleted, predict which fields the Census edit-and-imputation system filled in rather than the respondent reporting.

- **Input modality** — Fixed-width vector of ~124 integer/categorical survey codes per person (age, income, insurance, occupation, migration). No natural-language surface, no image; alphanumeric industry/occupation codes are integer-encoded.
- **Label** — A 77-bit allocation mask, one binary flag per variable, published in the same file. Framable per-flag (binary), as a joint mask (exact-match over 2^77), or as a count.
- **Corpus (G1)** — US Census Bureau ACS PUMS bulk files on www2.census.gov. VERIFIED BY DOWNLOAD THIS ROUND: csv_pca.zip (2023 1-Year, California) = 69,791,730 bytes, unpacks to psam_p06.csv with 392,318 person records, 287 columns, of which 77 are contiguous allocation flags (FAGEP..FYOEP). Flag rates are substantial and non-degenerate: FPINCP 0.275, FHICOVP 0.236, FPUBCOVP 0.229, FPRIVCOVP 0.225, FHINS7P 0.220, ... 56+ flags fire above 2%. One state-year alone is 3.9e5 units; 50 states x 20 years of 1-year files plus the 5-year files put the national corpus at ~1e7 person records and ~7.7e8 binary flag labels.
- **Manufacturer (G2, shape `c-generated-from-structured-source`)** — The Census Bureau's hot-deck edit-and-allocation system generated the released record from the raw questionnaire and RECORDS ITS OWN ACTIONS as the F* flag columns, published in the same CSV. Label manufacture is a column split: keep the 77 flag columns as y, delete them from X. Marginal cost is exactly zero - the labels ship inside the corpus, already paid for by the federal statistical system.
- **Scale argument (G3)** — The input is a vector of integer survey codes with no natural-language surface, so a frontier model cannot be prompted on it in any expressible way; even handed the codebook, the task is a joint-plausibility judgement over 77 correlated coded fields, not a reading task. The failure mode that killed the founder's last company - a bigger model with plain prompting matching a specialised technique - is not available to an attacker here. MEASURED SCALING on California, 80,000 held-out test records, 12 most common flags, HistGradientBoosting: n=1,000 mean AUC 0.8873 / joint 12-bit exact-match 0.6649 / Hamming err 0.1128; n=3,000 0.8981 / 0.6784 / 0.1078; n=10,000 0.9124 / 0.7087 / 0.1004; n=30,000 0.9201 / 0.7243 / 0.0971; n=100,000 0.9251 / 0.7280 / 0.0953. Monotone across two full orders of magnitude with no reversal; joint exact-match error falls 18.8% relative and Hamming error 15.5% relative over that span. HONEST WEAKNESS, STATED AT FULL SIZE: the curve is decelerating hard - the last half-order (30k to 100k) buys only +0.005 mean AUC and +0.004 exact-match, and a per-flag GBM will very likely saturate near 0.93-0.95 well before 1e6. This candidate's scaling story therefore has to live in the harder framings that were NOT measured: the full 77-bit joint mask, cross-state and cross-year transfer, and person-plus-household joint context. If those also flatten by 1e5, kill it rather than defend it. That test is a day's work and should be the first thing Phase 1 does.
- **Buyer (G5)** — National statistical offices and their commercial redistributors - the Census Bureau's own Disclosure Review Board, ONS and Eurostat equivalents, and social-science data archives such as IPUMS - who need to audit how much of a published table is real response versus machine fill before certifying it.
- **Why it survived the screen** — It is the only candidate in either round where A1, A2 and the scaling curve are all measured rather than argued, and where the dumb baseline was measured BELOW CHANCE. G2a is airtight and unusual: the manufacturer's private information (the raw questionnaire) is destroyed by federal law, so no amount of model scale can recover it from the student's side - this is not distillation, and there is no teacher to be a ceiling. G1 is verified by download with a permissive robots.txt (www2.census.gov serves 'User-agent: *' with no Disallow) and the bulk files are uncopyrighted federal works, so Phase 3 redistribution is clean. G6 is trivial: the entire probe above ran on this 4-core box in minutes, and 1e6 records is ~1GB as float32. The single reason not to lead with it would be the deceleration, which is real, measured, and stated - not hidden. CAVEAT TO CARRY FORWARD: the Census Data API terms of service contain a no-re-identification clause; the bulk PUMS zips are the clean path, and the adversarial 'privacy auditor' framing in the original pitch should be dropped because it walks toward that clause.

### 2. AssemblyProv - recovering the assembler, sequencing technology and read depth from a deposited genome

Given only the nucleotide sequence of a deposited prokaryotic genome assembly, predict the assembly software and version, the sequencing platform, and the read coverage that produced it.

- **Input modality** — Raw DNA sequence - a set of contigs as ACGTN strings plus their length multiset. Outside web-text pretraining distribution; compaction to contig-boundary windows plus a k-mer sketch is mandatory for the box.
- **Label** — The ##Genome-Assembly-Data## structured comment: Assembly Method (software plus version, e.g. 'SPAdes v. 3.15.5'), Sequencing Technology, and Genome Coverage as a continuous regression target.
- **Corpus (G1)** — NCBI GenBank via the Datasets API. VERIFIED BY BULK PULL THIS ROUND: 178,707 labelled GenBank bacterial assemblies retrieved across 10 taxa (S. aureus, E. coli, M. tuberculosis, S. Typhi, Pseudomonas, Acinetobacter, Enterococcus, Klebsiella, S. pneumoniae, Neisseria), every one carrying a non-empty assembly_method string. Label distribution measured: SPAdes 59,455 / SKESA 35,614 / Prokka 25,845 / Unicycler 8,328 / allpaths 6,340 / Velvet 4,430 / CLC NGS Cell 4,231 / SOAPdenovo 3,466, with 3,517 DISTINCT version strings. Ten taxa yield 1.8e5 labelled units, so the 3.8e6-assembly archive plausibly holds >1e6 labelled - and the label diversity (3,517 version strings) is the number that matters and it is large.
- **Manufacturer (G2, shape `c-generated-from-structured-source`)** — The assembler mechanically generated the contig set from a read set under a specific parameterisation, and the submitter's structured comment records the generator. Label manufacture is parsing the ##Genome-Assembly-Data## block out of the flatfile (or reading assembly_info from the Datasets API, as I did) and stripping it from the input. The corpus is self-labelling; marginal cost per label is one JSON field access.
- **Scale argument (G3)** — DNA sequence is not web text and a frontier model has no prompt-expressible route to 'which assembler built this contig set' - the input is megabases of ACGT. Scale monotonicity is plausible on mechanism: the discriminating evidence is rare local events (repeat-collapse boundaries, coverage-driven graph breaks, the exact position where the assembler gave up), each assembly contributes a handful, and the label space is large (3,517 measured version strings), so characterising the tail genuinely needs volume rather than saturating on the first thousand genomes. The threat is not a bigger model; the threat is QUAST plus a decision tree, and I have now measured exactly how much that threat gets.
- **Buyer (G5)** — Biosecurity and sequence-provenance auditors - IBBIS, national biosecurity agencies, and DNA synthesis screening firms - plus journal and repository data-integrity teams who need to check whether a deposited assembly's declared provenance is consistent with the sequence itself.
- **Why it survived the screen** — It survives because the measurement I ran came back the way the candidate needed and against the pool author's own pessimism: the free-statistics baseline does NOT take the fine-grained label. G2a is genuine and non-invertible - assembly is lossy, unlike the NMR, cryo-EM and halftone candidates that died to closed-form inverses. G4 is the whitest in the pool: dblp returns 0 for every formulation I tried, GitHub returns 0 repositories, HuggingFace returns 0 datasets, and the only adjacent item anywhere is a sequence-forensics preprint about RaTG13's sample type, which is a different label. G1 is verified at 1.8e5 labelled units from 10 taxa out of a 3.8e6 archive. THE ONE THING STILL UNMEASURED IS THE THING THE WHOLE CANDIDATE RESTS ON: whether the NUCLEOTIDES carry signal beyond the contig-length multiset. It is possible that everything is in the lengths, in which case this collapses into the dumb baseline I just measured. That test - pull ~500 assemblies from two assemblers at matched taxon and N50, and ask whether contig-boundary sequence adds over lengths alone - is one afternoon and ~2GB, and it must be run BEFORE anything else. TWO FLAGS TO CARRY: ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov, api.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov and eutils.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov all serve robots.txt 'User-agent: * / Disallow: /' (I checked all three), which is the Maven Central shape - but unlike Maven Central there is no ai-train Content-Signal, NCBI publishes a sanctioned programmatic-access policy (<=3 requests/second, bulk runs off-peak, use the eutils host) and states 'NCBI itself places no restrictions on the use or distribution of the data contained therein', so the documented API is the legal route and crawling is not. Second, NCBI warns submitters may assert IP claims it cannot adjudicate, so Phase 3 should ship accession lists plus a fetch script, not repackaged sequence.

### 3. EphemErr - GNSS broadcast-ephemeris error nowcasting

From the broadcast navigation message alone - what every GNSS receiver on Earth already holds - predict how wrong that message will turn out to be, with the post-processed precise ephemeris as truth.

- **Input modality** — The broadcast navigation record: Keplerian element set plus clock polynomial (af0/af1/af2) plus IODE/URA-SISA/health flags, ~16 numeric fields, plus geometry derivable from it (sun-satellite-Earth angle, eclipse/yaw-maneuver flag, seconds since toe/upload). ~100 floats per unit, non-text, non-image.
- **Label** — Per-epoch radial/along-track/cross-track orbit error and clock error of that broadcast record against the precise SP3/CLK product over its validity window. The primary target is the conditional UPPER QUANTILE of SISRE, not a point estimate.
- **Corpus (G1)** — Daily merged broadcast navigation files (BRDC RINEX) joined to precise orbit/clock products (SP3/CLK) by satellite and epoch. ~110 active satellites x ~12 nav records/day x a decade is ~4.8e6 (record, error-trajectory) pairs and ~1e8 satellite-epochs at 5-minute label granularity. Storage is ~10-20 GB for a decade of raw and a few hundred MB after feature reduction. VERIFIED THIS ROUND: HuggingFace returns 0 datasets for 'GNSS ephemeris'/'broadcast ephemeris'/'SP3 orbit'/'RINEX' shaped this way (the 8 GNSS hits are RTK NMEA logs, jamming recordings and SLAM evaluation sets), and GitHub returns 0 repositories. NOT VERIFIED THIS ROUND: I did not download a BRDC/SP3 pair. NASA CDDIS robots.txt is 'Disallow: /archive' and BKG's mirror is 'Disallow: /root_ftp/' - neither may be crawled; the sanctioned routes are CDDIS's Earthdata-authenticated HTTPS and ESA GSSC (robots.txt permissive, only /wp-admin/), and I confirmed gssc.esa.int and navigation-office.esa.int return 200 but did not locate the exact daily-archive path.
- **Manufacturer (G2, shape `b-environment-free-truth`)** — Propagate each broadcast record's Keplerian and clock model to a grid of epochs, difference against the precise SP3/CLK at the same epochs in the same frame with antenna-phase-centre and time-system offsets applied, emit the error trajectory. One-time download of a few GB, then zero marginal cost per label forever. This is exactly the differencing that produces the published SISRE statistics the constellations report.
- **Scale argument (G3)** — The input is a binary/numeric orbital-element block. A frontier LLM with plain prompting cannot express Keplerian propagation, ECEF frame rotation and error-quantile estimation over it - the task is not merely hard for prompting, it is not expressible, which is precisely the property the founder needs after his last company was matched by plain prompting on a 3x model. Scale monotonicity has a clean mechanism: the payload is a rare-event tail (bad uploads, clock jumps, eclipse-season yaw, maneuvers), so the quantile estimate at 99.9% is data-starved by construction and should keep improving with years and constellations long after the mean saturates. This is the one candidate whose target metric is intrinsically tail-shaped, which is the best available defence against the flattening that is visibly threatening AllocTrace.
- **Buyer (G5)** — GNSS correction and integrity service providers - Swift Navigation, Point One Navigation, Trimble RTX, u-blox PointPerfect - who sell tighter protection levels to autonomous-vehicle and precision-agriculture customers, plus ARAIM Integrity Support Message generators at EUSPA and the FAA.
- **Why it survived the screen** — It survives on the two gates that killed most of the pool. G2a is the cleanest in the whole set and is the closest structural analogue to VPT anywhere here: the manufacturer literally observes the future the student is being asked to predict, reconstructed from a global network the student has no access to, and no scaling of a general model closes that gap. G4 is genuinely white and I measured it: dblp returns 2 hits for 'broadcast ephemeris error' and both are characterisation studies (Galileo error analysis 2017-2020; real-time PPP), 1 hit for 'signal in space range error' (a pseudolite paper), 0 for 'GNSS integrity machine learning'; arXiv's only SISRE hit is a BeiDou-3 quality assessment; no open dataset and no GitHub repo. G6 is ideal - ~100-float units, four rungs in minutes to hours on 4 cores with no GPU. G1 has a clean redistribution path that most of this pool lacks: GPS broadcast navigation messages and NGA precise GPS ephemerides are US Government works with no copyright, so a GPS-only shipped artifact is safe, and Galileo/BeiDou can be added later after their own terms check. It ranks third only because everything about its A2 is argument rather than measurement, and today's Sentinel-1 result is a reminder of what happens to unmeasured A2 arguments in this exact structure - smooth-physics domains where the incumbent is a fitted curve.

### 4. ModelPrint-CMIP - recovering the source climate model from a gridded field

Given a single gridded output field with all CF metadata deleted and every model regridded to a common grid, predict which CMIP6 model, experiment and physics variant generated it.

- **Input modality** — 2-D/3-D floating-point geophysical field on a common lat-lon grid, coarsened and stored as float16. Squarely outside web-text distribution.
- **Label** — CMIP6 DRS attributes: source_id (model, ~100 classes), experiment_id, and the realisation/physics variant label.
- **Corpus (G1)** — The ESGF CMIP6 archive - ~100 models across thousands of variable/experiment/member combinations, petabyte scale, so daily field slices certainly exceed 1e6. NOT VERIFIED THIS ROUND: I did not query ESGF and did not enumerate the per-model licence split, and both are load-bearing.
- **Manufacturer (G2, shape `c-generated-from-structured-source`)** — The GCM generated the field under a fully recorded configuration preserved in the NetCDF global attributes and the DRS path. Label manufacture is deleting the attributes and keeping the numbers - zero marginal cost across the whole archive.
- **Scale argument (G3)** — Numeric geophysical fields are not prompt-expressible and a frontier text model has no route in. Distinguishing ~100 models by their internal variability structure plausibly benefits from far more samples than any single published study has used, and the label space (100 models x experiments x variants) is large.
- **Buyer (G5)** — Climate-risk analytics vendors and reinsurers - Jupiter Intelligence, Munich Re - who receive downscaled or vendor-processed climate fields with provenance stripped and need to verify which model ensemble they actually bought.
- **Why it survived the screen** — It survives literally rather than comfortably, and it is included as a hedge, not a recommendation. It passes every hard gate as written: G2a is real, A2 is weak after regridding (rare in this pool), A1 is established non-zero by prior work rather than assumed, the input is numeric so G3's frontier-model failure mode is unavailable, and G4 returns exactly one serious effort, which is under the ~3 threshold. THREE HONEST REASONS IT RANKS LAST, STATED AT FULL SIZE. First, that one effort - 'Using artificial intelligence to identify CMIP6 models from daily SLP maps', npj Climate and Atmospheric Science 2025, which I confirmed via Crossref this round - IS the task. It is not adjacent. A funder's first question will be what this adds, and the only honest answer is 'the scaling curve, which they did not produce'. Second, that same paper reports the result collapsing seasonally (strong in summer, sharply worse in winter), which says the ceiling is set by irreducible internal variability rather than by data volume - the flattening risk that the founder specifically cannot afford, and here it is already documented in the literature rather than merely suspected. Third, G1 is the weakest of the four survivors: CMIP6 licensing is mixed and a non-trivial minority of modelling groups use CC-BY-NC, which is incompatible with both a commercial thesis and a shipped open artifact, so the corpus must be filtered per source_id before anything is redistributed - and I did not verify that split. Promote this only if the AllocTrace joint-mask curve flattens and the AssemblyProv nucleotide test fails.

“I did not find anything” is not evidence of white space. Every search below was actually
run, and what came back is recorded whether or not it was convenient.

### G4 detail — EphemErr — GNSS broadcast-ephemeris error nowcasting (predict per-record orbit/clock error and the conditional upper quantile of SISRE from the broadcast navigation message, with post-processed SP3/CLK as truth)

**Verdict: FAIL** (crowding 9/10)

G4 fails on both clauses, and it fails because the round-1 reconnaissance searched the wrong indexes. dblp and arXiv genuinely return almost nothing here — but that is a property of dblp's CS scope and of a community that does not preprint, not a property of the field. The moment I queried the navigation community's own publishers directly (Crossref DOI prefix 10.33012, which is the Institute of Navigation; the GPS Solutions journal endpoint; Europe PMC, which indexes the MDPI Sensors/Remote Sensing corpus where this field actually publishes), the white space evaporated.

EFFORT COUNT: at least seven serious efforts, in two distinct clusters, against a PASS threshold of three.

Cluster 1 — the candidate's PRIMARY target (conditional upper quantile of SISRE) is the subject of a continuous, standardised, aviation-certification-grade research programme running 2010-2025 under the name SISRE overbounding / ARAIM Integrity Support Message generation. I pulled the full abstract of the nearest item, ION GNSS+ 2022 DOI 10.33012/2022.18306, and it does not merely characterise the aggregate: it bootstraps the distribution of the MAXIMUM projected clock+ephemeris error and explicitly 'partition[s] the data for different observable conditions ... to determine whether the current bounding behavior is representative of future data.' That is conditional upper-quantile estimation of SISRE, performed by stratification instead of by a learned model. Around it sit ION ITM 2023 (18606), ION GNSS+ 2020 (17577), ION GNSS+ 2019 (16938), NAVIGATION 2020 (navi.346), IEEE TAES 2023, IEEE/ION PLANS 2020, and GPS Solutions 2023 (s10291-023-01525-2). The incumbent is not a gap; it is a deployed standard with a working group attached.

Cluster 2 — learned per-satellite modelling of broadcast ephemeris error is independently replicated by at least five groups since 2021: Remote Sensing 2021 (BP/PSO-BP, 30-50% RMS3D improvement), Comput. Intell. Neurosci. 2022 (BDS-3 PSO+BP), Sensors 2022 (LSTM on IGS RTS orbit corrections specifically across the IOD/upload boundary — which is the exact feature EphemErr proposes to exploit), SPIE ICGRSM 2024, IEEE ICAACE 2026, plus a 2024 RNN preprint. Sensors 2022 is the most damaging: it is a learned model, conditioned on the upload discontinuity, predicting the correction to the broadcast orbit.

OPEN DATASET: the second clause also fails. BDSet (Scientific Data 2026, DOI 10.1038/s41597-026-07032-6) is an open, DOI'd, continuously-updated 142.7 GB / ~255 million-message corpus of decoded PPP-B2b SSR corrections — per-satellite orbit and clock corrections to the BDS broadcast ephemeris, i.e. a close proxy for the candidate's label at well above 10^6 pairs. It is not identical (real-time ground-segment estimate rather than post-processed SP3 truth, BDS only), so on its own it would argue MARGINAL. Combined with the effort count it does not rescue the verdict.

TWO FURTHER PROBLEMS I HIT WHILE VERIFYING, WORTH RECORDING EVEN THOUGH G4 WAS MY REMIT:

A2 (heuristic-shaped labels) looks fatal independently. The dumbest non-learned baseline here is a lookup table keyed on two fields that are already IN the broadcast record: the URA/SISA index — a field whose entire designed purpose is to declare the expected magnitude of this exact error — and age-of-data since toe. Error growth with age-of-data is the single best-characterised effect in the literature. A per-(satellite-block, URA-index, AOD-bin) empirical quantile table would capture most of the signal, and the ARAIM ISM pipeline already builds precisely that table. The ION 2022 result that 'most of the standard deviations are below the user range accuracy' is a direct measurement that the broadcast URA field alone already overbounds the error most of the time. This is the COBOL 50-line-baseline failure mode, and the incumbent baseline here is not a strawman I would have to build — it is certified and deployed.

G2 moat: the label manufacturer is commodity tooling. RTKLIB and GNSSTk both ship broadcast-ephemeris evaluation and SP3-c precise interpolation; the EUREF/EPN community publishes routine broadcast-vs-precise runs; GPS Solutions 2018 already reports an 18-year BRDC-vs-precise study across four IGS data centres. The differencing pipeline is an afternoon's work for anyone in the field, which means no data moat accrues from building it.

G2a (information asymmetry) is the one gate the candidate passes cleanly and it is genuinely well-argued — the manufacturer sees the reconstructed truth from a ~500-station network days later, the student sees only the forward prediction. That asymmetry is real. It is simply not enough when the resulting problem is already owned twice over.

RECOMMENDATION: kill. Do not attempt to rescue this by narrowing the framing to 'learned conditional quantile, nav-record-only input, at 10^6 scale.' That delta is a method contribution inside an occupied, standardised problem, not a domain with white space — and the aviation buyer, the one with real money, cannot accept a learned tail bound in place of a certified statistical one, so the method delta and the economic tail point in opposite directions.

### G4 detail — ModelPrint-CMIP — recovering source_id / experiment_id / variant_label of a CMIP6 climate model from a metadata-stripped, regridded gridded geophysical field

**Verdict: FAIL** (crowding 8/10)

Three independent serious efforts do precisely this task, and the composition is worse than the count. (1) Brunner & Sippel, Environmental Data Science 2023, run 47-way classification over 43 CMIP6 models from single daily metadata-stripped temperature maps — the proposed task at nearly the proposed class cardinality — and release MIT-licensed code plus PRE-TRAINED classifiers and a Zenodo deposit. (2) Yiou & Thao, npj Climate and Atmospheric Science 2025, is a Nature-family paper published this year whose title is a near-verbatim restatement of the proposed headline: 'Using artificial intelligence to identify CMIP6 models from daily SLP maps'. It even harvests the model-family structure from the classifier's confusions, which is the genealogy angle. (3) Labe & Barnes, Earth and Space Science 2022, founded the framing with a 7-GCM Arctic classifier plus LRP. A reviewer does not need to search; the near-duplicate is in a Nature journal from the current year.

Strict arithmetic puts this at exactly the 3-effort MARGINAL boundary, and no open dataset ships 10^6 labelled (field, source_id) pairs under that exact label. I am calling FAIL anyway for three measured reasons.

First, the shipped-artifact contribution is already built. ClimDetect (HuggingFace, CC-BY-4.0, 1,272,212 rows) is CMIP6 daily fields regridded to a uniform 64x128 grid with provenance stripped down to (input, target, year) — that is the proposed corpus construction, at the proposed scale, already open, with the CMIP6 model list shipped alongside as Clim_Detect_ds_info.csv and the pipeline released. ClimateSet (NeurIPS 2023 D&B, 36 models) is organised by source model, so labelled pairs fall out for free. Phase 3's open artifact would be a groupby on someone else's release.

Second, the incumbents' own numbers show the quality curve saturates orders of magnitude below rung 4, which is the thing the founder cannot be caught shipping. Brunner & Sippel get 99.4% on the binary task with LOGISTIC REGRESSION — a linear model is the dumbest learned baseline there is, and it nearly solves it. Labe & Barnes hit 100% test accuracy with a 2,016 -> 10 -> 10 -> 7 MLP on 840 training maps. Brunner & Sippel themselves write that more samples 'would SLOWLY improve the classification skill even further' — the incumbents have stated the scaling hypothesis and pre-characterised it as flat. The 47-class daily version at 83.4% leaves headroom, so the curve is not provably dead, but a study whose deliverable IS the scaling curve cannot start from a lane where a linear model already scores 99.4% at 2k samples per class.

Third, the buyer story does not survive the practitioner check. CF conventions plus the CMIP6 'ripf' variant identifier uniquely tag every ensemble member, ES-DOC provides standardised model documentation and an errata service, and ESGF publishes checksums. Orphaned-provenance is solved administratively and exactly, not probabilistically — I found no practitioner thread describing it as an open problem.

Two secondary notes for the record. G1 is unresolved and is a real risk: CMIP6 defaults to CC BY-SA 4.0 per modelling centre with some centres on CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, and the SHARE-ALIKE clause propagates to derivatives, so a mixed-source corpus inherits the most restrictive terms. I could not measure the per-model split because esgf-node.llnl.gov/esg-search now 302-redirects to esgf-node.ornl.gov/esgf-1-5-bridge. Separately, G2a is weaker than the brief claims: the ensemble-member/initial-condition half of the label is unrecoverable in principle from a single slice, which is not information asymmetry, it is an unlearnable target — the manufacturer knows something the student cannot ever recover, so that portion of the label is noise, not signal, and it caps the achievable curve rather than lifting it. Only source_id is genuinely learnable, and that is the exact 47-class problem Brunner & Sippel published in 2023.

Searched 15 formulations across 14 venues, including three domain-specific ones (PCMDI/WCRP CMIP6 standards, the EGU General Assembly programme via Copernicus meetingorganizer, and the ESGF data infrastructure) plus the field's own journals (Environmental Data Science, npj Climate and Atmospheric Science, Earth and Space Science, GRL, Earth System Dynamics, GMD). arXiv and dblp returned literal zeros, which initially reads as white space but is an artifact of where this community publishes — the work is all in Copernicus, AGU and Cambridge venues, and it is there.

## Round 2: adversarial review — 2 finalists, default posture REJECT

Each finalist was handed to an agent instructed to kill it, with the founder's previous
failure stated explicitly as the thing to look for. Several of these reviews are backed by
measurements the reviewer took on this box rather than by argument.

| Finalist | G3 | G1 | G6 | Verdict |
|---|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| AllocTrace — recovering the Census edit-and-imputation (allocation) ma | FAIL | PASS | PASS | **REJECT** |
| AssemblyProv — recovering assembler software/version, sequencing techn | FAIL | PASS | FAIL | **REJECT** |

### Adversarial review — AllocTrace — recovering the Census edit-and-imputation (allocation) mask from released ACS PUMS person microdata

**Recommendation: REJECT.** G3 FAIL · G1 PASS · G6 PASS. Reviewer confidence: High. Every decisive claim is a measurement on the real corpus, not an argument: 392,318 California person records plus Oregon 2019 and 2023 downloaded and processed this session, a household-grouped split so no household spans train and test, and a documented per-term decomposition of the dumb rule (the NW* terms are confounded with age<16 at r = -0.44 to -0.88 and I report that; ANC==4 is confounded at only r = +0.076 and carries the result alone). The G2a falsification is a direct quotation from the official Census data dictionary, not an inference. The one number I did NOT measure is the extrapolated asymptote (0.958 leaky / 0.865 de-leaked), which is a geometric fit to three measured decades and is labelled as such; the fit predicted the 3.06e5 rungs to within 0.003 and 0.012 before they landed. Two residual uncertainties, neither of which changes the verdict: I did not test the person-plus-household joint framing the proposal names as its fallback (it would add correlated-unit signal but also demands the household-grouped split I already used, and it does not touch the G5 hole), and Semantic Scholar rate-limited me twice so my white-space evidence leans on Crossref, arXiv, dblp, Europe PMC and HuggingFace. A NARROWED version does exist and I want it recorded fairly — "hot-deck footprint detection", i.e. the de-leaked task with all ten nonresponse markers deleted — and it would genuinely PASS G2a and A2 and has a livelier curve than the full version. I still reject it, because it dies on the same G5 wall (both the Census and IPUMS publish the label for free, everywhere) and because its honest numbers are AUC 0.82 and a joint exact-match tied with a constant, which is not a fundable scaling curve.

**G3 — kill attempt**

Two attacks. (A) DELETE THE LEAK: retrain HistGradientBoosting with the ten published nonresponse-marker columns removed (ANC, ANC1P, ANC2P, WRK, NWAB, NWAV, NWLA, NWLK, NWRE, plus ESR/MSP/OC/RC/PAOC/NOP/DECADE), household-grouped split, six representative flags. Leaky model: 0.9082 / 0.9342 / 0.9467 / 0.9492 mean AUC at n = 1e3 / 1e4 / 1e5 / 3.06e5. De-leaked model: 0.6821 / 0.7635 / 0.8087 / 0.8202 at the same rungs — it collapses by 0.226 AUC at n=1e3. Almost the entire advertised result is the leak. (B) FIT THE CURVE: leaky per-decade increments decay at rho=0.481, giving a geometric tail of +0.0116 beyond 1e5 and an asymptote near 0.958 — meaning the curve is 97.5% COMPLETE AT n=1e5, before the first rung of the intended 1e6 range; the last half-decade (1e5 to 3.06e5) buys +0.0025. The de-leaked curve decays at rho=0.555, asymptote ~0.865, 84.5% complete at 1e5, and its half-decade 1e5 to 3.06e5 buys +0.0115. Worse, the de-leaked model's joint 6-bit exact-match at 306,250 training records is 0.6081 — statistically indistinguishable from the constant predictor's 0.6073, i.e. after a third of a million examples it still cannot beat "always predict not-allocated" on the joint mask. (C) TRANSFER: I downloaded Oregon 1-Year PUMS for 2019 and 2023 and tested cross-state and cross-year. CA2023 100k -> OR2023 = 0.9383, CA2023 100k -> OR2019 = 0.9281, OR2019 40k -> OR2023 = 0.9318, versus OR2023 30k -> OR2023 held-out = 0.9699. Transfer is nearly free, which is superficially good for the abundance claim but is further evidence the signal is a generic low-dimensional nonresponse-propensity latent, not a rich dataset-specific hot-deck footprint that would reward 1e6 examples.

**G3 — does it survive?**

Two things survive honestly and I want them on the record. First, the proposal is RIGHT that a frontier model with plain prompting cannot eat this: the input is 130 integer survey codes with no natural-language surface, and the founder's specific historical failure mode is genuinely unavailable to an attacker here. The attacker that actually kills it is not a bigger model, it is `sum of eight equality tests`. Second, there IS a real residual signal once the leak is removed — the de-leaked curve rises monotonically 0.6821 -> 0.7635 -> 0.8087 -> 0.8202 with no reversal, and its per-decade increments (+0.081, +0.045) are LIVELIER than the leaky model's (+0.026, +0.013). That residual is the genuinely FDM-1-shaped part of the idea: joint implausibility of a hot-deck donor value against the rest of the record. But it is still 84.5% exhausted at 1e5, it cannot beat a constant on the joint mask, and it is a much smaller effect than the headline. G3 fails on headroom, not on the prompting argument.

**G1 — licence reality**

PASS — this is the candidate's one unambiguous strength and it is real. https://www2.census.gov/robots.txt returns `User-agent: *` with NO Disallow line (only named crawlers RavenCrawler/MegaIndex/BLP_bbot/TweetmemeBot are blocked, plus crawl-delays for Applebot/Googlebot/Slurp/bingbot); there is no Content-Signal header and no ai-train restriction — the opposite of the Maven Central finding that killed the round-1 bytecode candidate. I verified live fetches: HTTP 200 with content-type application/zip on https://www2.census.gov/programs-surveys/acs/data/pums/2023/1-Year/csv_pwy.zip, and I actually downloaded and unpacked 2015/2019/2023 Oregon (csv_por.zip) plus the 2023 California file already on disk (69,791,730 bytes, 392,318 person records, 287 columns, 77 contiguous allocation flags FAGEP..FYOEP). These are US federal government works and therefore carry no copyright under 17 USC 105; IPUMS, NBER and others redistribute PUMS routinely. The only restrictive terms I found are on the Census Data API (https://www.census.gov/data/developers/about/terms-of-service.html): a re-identification prohibition ("will not use these data, alone or in combination with any other Census or non-Census data, to identify any individual person, household, business or other entity") and an attribution notice requirement. Neither bars AI training nor redistribution, and neither governs the www2 bulk files. Shipping an open artifact in Phase 3 is legally clean.

**G2 — label noise and degeneracy**

COULD NOT VERIFY

**G6 — CPU feasibility, concretely**

Not the constraint; comfortably within the 4-core/15GB/no-GPU/30GB box. Measured on the real file: 130 feature codes at int16 = 260 B/unit (ADJINC needs int32, trivially handled), plus a packed 77-bit mask at 10 B = 270 B/unit. Corpus at 1e3/1e4/1e5/1e6/1e7 units = 0.3 MB / 2.7 MB / 27 MB / 270 MB / 2.7 GB, all resident in 15 GB. Raw bulk is ~24 GB zipped for 50 states x 20 years, which is tight against ~22 GB free but streams state-by-state. Empirically, this session trained 6 HistGradientBoosting heads x 4 rungs up to n=306,250 on 130 features in roughly 25 minutes on 2 threads while two other agents' jobs were saturating the box (load average 10+ on 4 cores). Scaling to 77 heads x 4 rungs x 1e6 is a small number of CPU-hours. If this candidate died, it did not die on G6.

**Strongest case against**

The advertised result is leakage, and the leak is not subtle — it is printed in the codebook. The proposal's G2a sentence asserts the student sees a record "in which every field is populated and internally consistent"; the official 2023 ACS PUMS Data Dictionary says otherwise for ten of the 130 input columns, five of which the dictionary itself stamps "(UNEDITED)". Because ACS never imputes ancestry (FANCP fires on 0 of 392,318 records) it simply publishes ANC=4 ".Not reported" verbatim, and because item nonresponse clusters within respondent, that single published code predicts allocation of OTHER items at mean AUC 0.8026 with a one-line rule and zero training data — inside the proposal's own quoted "learned 0.886–0.939" band on several flags (FESRP 0.9439 for the eight-line version). Delete those ten columns and the model falls from 0.9082 to 0.6821 at n=1e3, and at n=306,250 its joint 6-bit exact-match is 0.6081 against a constant predictor's 0.6073 — a third of a million examples cannot beat "always predict not-allocated". Meanwhile the leaky curve is 97.5% complete at n=1e5 and the de-leaked curve 84.5% complete, so the two orders of magnitude the thesis is built on buy roughly +0.01 and +0.05 AUC respectively. And the economic tail is empty in a way that cannot be engineered around: the label is a published column in the same free CSV, IPUMS already ships equivalent Q-flags for every sample back to 1850 and manufactures them where they never existed, and White (2015) shows that when a statistical agency actually needs missing imputation flags it recovers the archived file rather than training a predictor. The proposal deserves credit for stating its own weakness honestly ("the curve is decelerating hard... if those also flatten by 1e5, kill it rather than defend it") — I ran exactly that test a day early, and they flatten.

### Adversarial review — AssemblyProv — recovering assembler software/version, sequencing technology and read coverage from a deposited prokaryotic genome assembly (NCBI GenBank)

**Recommendation: REJECT.** G3 FAIL · G1 PASS · G6 FAIL. Reviewer confidence: High on the fine-grained targets (version, technology, coverage): the kill rests on a primary-source changelog, a same-software negative control that scores as high as the real pairs, a fake-label control, and a temporal split where the model loses to a constant — four independent lines that agree. High on G6 (measured bytes, arithmetic). Medium-high on the domain as a whole: the family-only narrowing has real but thin capacity, and its buyer already owns the free tool that captures most of it. Medium on my one untested flank, the base-level sequence, for the reason stated above. Note also that a narrowed AssemblyProv-family study would still have to publish its headline as a ~20-class balanced accuracy under a submitter-grouped split, against a free incumbent at 0.688 ACC, over a corpus whose label information saturates at ~10^5 independent groups — that is not a scaling-curve story, it is a 1.5-order-of-magnitude story with a strong non-learned competitor, so I do not recommend it even as a backup.

**G3 — kill attempt**

Three independent attacks, all on real data. (a) SPLIT LEAK. The proposer's numbers come from a random split, but the label is a project-level constant: 97.4% of the 5,918 S. aureus bioprojects carry exactly ONE method string, and "predict method = most common method of this bioproject" scores 0.9553 with zero sequence involved; release-month alone scores 0.6867 against a 0.4797 majority. Reproducing their setting with a RandomForest on 59,865 assemblies I get random-split family ACC 0.7505 / balACC 0.3870 (they report 0.745 / 0.461 — same instrument). Switching only the split: bioproject-grouped 0.5187 / 0.2176; full-method-string random 0.7117 -> grouped 0.3892; and on a temporal split the full-method model scores 0.7630 against a train-majority constant at 0.7770 — it LOSES to a constant. (b) EFFECTIVE N. ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/genomes/genbank/bacteria/assembly_summary.txt has 3,271,826 rows but only 122,550 distinct bioprojects; the single largest (PRJNA514245, NCBI Pathogen Detection) is 660,991 assemblies = 20% of the entire archive under one method string, and the top 8 projects are 41% of it. For the "defensible target": SPAdes v. 3.13.1 = 1,130 assemblies from 17 bioprojects; "Spades v. 3.14" = 225 assemblies from ONE. The label carries ~10^5 independent draws, not 10^6, and the 1M rung is pure within-lab replication. (c) FRONTIER MODEL. This attack fails — the input really is megabases of ACGT, and plain prompting is not expressible over it. G3 dies on (a) and (b), not on (c).

**G3 — does it survive?**

Only one thing survives: the assembler FAMILY task (SPAdes vs SKESA vs Canu vs Flye vs ALLPATHS...) has genuinely non-zero capacity — different assemblers really do impose different contig structure, and a submitter-grouped model on length statistics alone reaches 0.3813 ACC over 20 classes. But the free incumbent that already exploits it reaches 0.6883, the buyer holds that incumbent, and the residual lives in rare assemblers with single-digit bioproject counts. Version, technology and coverage do not survive: version is inside the confound floor by measurement and by changelog, technology is 93% Illumina (the proposer already conceded this), and coverage is a human-rounded typed number correlating 0.414 with the assembly's own realised depth.

**G1 — licence reality**

PASS, and it is the only clean pass. NCBI Website and Data Usage Policies (https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/home/about/policies/), Molecular Data Usage section, verbatim: "NCBI itself places no restrictions on the use or distribution of the data contained therein. Nor do we accept data when the submitter has requested restrictions on reuse or redistribution." Redistribution of a derived open dataset is therefore permitted. Count verified by bulk pull, not by claim: ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/genomes/genbank/bacteria/assembly_summary.txt = 3,271,826 assembly rows. I pulled 60,000 S. aureus + 38,996 E. coli + ~30,000 K. pneumoniae records live; 59,882/60,000 (99.8%) carry a non-empty assembly_method. No Content-Signal header on www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov (checked; Maven Central's ai-train=no has no analogue here). One caveat to state honestly rather than hide: https://api.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/robots.txt is "User-agent: * / Disallow: /" — the standard NCBI back-end crawler exclusion. Bulk access is intended to go through ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov and the documented Datasets API/CLI, and the data-usage policy is explicit and controlling, so I read this as a crawler directive rather than an ai-train prohibition — but a careful Phase 3 should pull via FTP, not by crawling the API host.

**G2 — label noise and degeneracy**

COULD NOT VERIFY

**G6 — CPU feasibility, concretely**

Measured over 41 real Datasets-API downloads: mean 829 KB, median 827 KB of compressed FASTA per assembly. Rungs: 1k = 0.8 GB, 10k = 8.3 GB, 100k = 83 GB, 1M = 829 GB, against a 30 GB disk. Two-bit packing does not save it — mean S. aureus assembly is 2.85 Mbp = 0.71 MB packed, so 100k = 71 GB and 1M = 713 GB. The 100k rung is already 2.4-2.8x over disk and the 1M rung is 24-28x over, so half the required scaling curve cannot be built on the stated box with the stated input ("contigs as ACGTN strings"). The only representation that does fit is the compaction the proposer calls mandatory — a contig-length list is 0.2 KB/genome, 0.2 GB at 1M — and that is exactly the representation I tested and found to sit inside the confound floor for the fine label. So the box forces the representation that has no measured capacity, and the representation that might have capacity does not fit the box. (Streaming-and-discarding would mean fixing hand-designed features in advance, which contradicts the sequence-model thesis, and re-pulling ~800 GB from NCBI per epoch is not a viable training loop.)

**Strongest case against**

The strongest case for selecting it anyway, which I want on the record because it is the one thing my kill does not fully close: my A1 measurement used the contig-LENGTH multiset, not the bases. The published mechanism for a non-zero channel (Rahman & Pachter, Genome Research 2022, on unsafe-unitig misassembly and bidirected-graph underassembly) lives in base-level structure — repeat-collapse boundaries, the exact junction where the assembler gave up — which a length multiset cannot see. A sequence model over raw bases could in principle find signal I did not test for. Three things blunt this. First, the changelog evidence is representation-independent: if SPAdes 3.13.0 and 3.13.1 execute the identical code on a bacterial isolate, no representation recovers a difference that was never written. Second, the effective-N result (122,550 bioprojects, 97.4% single-method, top 8 projects = 41% of the archive) and the temporal-split result (model loses to a constant) are also representation-independent. Third, testing the rebuttal costs 83-829 GB on a 30 GB box. If someone insists, the cheap decisive experiment is ~500 SPAdes 3.13.0 and ~500 SPAdes 3.13.1 bacterial assemblies from disjoint submitters, full FASTA, any sequence model, submitter-grouped: the changelog predicts 0.500, and I measured 0.566 +- 0.069 from lengths. A second, more honest fallback exists — install two SPAdes versions and assemble one public read set with each, the direct javac-345/345 test — but SPAdes 3.13.x on 4 cores / 15 GB is hours per run and I judged the changelog plus the negative control to be the stronger evidence per hour.

## Round 2: known limitations of this round

- The round-2 pipeline returned the adversarial payload for surviving candidates but did not carry their G4 payload through the pipeline stage, so the recorded g4_results contain only the two candidates whose G4 verdict was FAIL (EphemErr, ModelPrint-CMIP). The G4 verdicts for AllocTrace and AssemblyProv are referenced inside their adversarial reviews but their full search records were not returned. This is a harness defect, recorded rather than hidden.
- AssemblyProv's adversary explicitly recorded the one thing its kill does not close: the A1 measurement used the contig-LENGTH multiset, not the bases, and the published mechanism for a non-zero channel lives in base-level structure. It also named the cheap decisive experiment (~500 SPAdes 3.13.0 vs ~500 3.13.1 bacterial assemblies from disjoint submitters, submitter-grouped) and the reason it was not run (83-829 GB against a 30 GB disk).
- The AllocTrace 'de-leaked' residual is real and rises monotonically (0.6821 -> 0.7635 -> 0.8087 -> 0.8202 mean AUC across 1e3/1e4/1e5/3.06e5) with livelier per-decade increments than the leaky model. It was rejected on headroom and on an empty economic tail, not on the absence of signal.


---

# Round 3

### Round 3: scout 1 — honest read

> Scouts were told that “nothing in this structure threads it, and here is why” is a
> valid and valuable answer, and is better than padding.

```
NOTHING HERE THREADS THE BIND. I did not find a candidate I would defend through adversarial review, and I recommend that this arm contributes zero SELECTs.

WHAT I ACTUALLY MEASURED (all from NASDAQ TotalView-ITCH 5.0, 2019-01-30, first 400 MB of the 4.76 GB file = 31,627,545 messages parsed to 09:45:00 ET; scripts and the book snapshot are at /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-raise-v1/5d675fa9-5fc4-501c-b3b3-a76510397d00/scratchpad/itch/):

1. Order-book snapshot at 09:45:00 ET: 1,549,395 live displayed orders across 656,309 (symbol, side, price) levels. Recovering the order-count decomposition of an aggregated level: H(n_orders) = 1.7993 bits, H(n_orders | total shares) = 0.9301 bits, I = 0.8692 bits. On the 253,568 levels with >=300 shares: H = 2.8272, H|shares = 1.6332, I = 1.1940 bits. F1 PASSES. But the dumbest rules are strong: always-answer-1 = 68.08%, floor(shares/100) = 61.10%, and an empirical Bayes LOOKUP on the share count alone — one dict, zero training — = 75.86%. F2 MARGINAL, the round-2 census shape.

2. Hidden (non-displayed) liquidity, 09:30:00-09:45:00 ET: 13,242,796 hidden-executed shares against 72,606,429 displayed = 15.43% of volume. Across the 1,454 symbols trading >=5,000 shares, the hidden fraction runs p10 0.018 / median 0.133 / p90 0.372, mean 0.173, sd 0.150. The dumbest rule — give every symbol the market-wide fraction — scores R^2 = -0.0149. NEGATIVE. Worse than the cross-sectional mean.

That second result matters more than the candidate does. DarkFill passes F1 and F2 decisively, has a perfect G2a (the exchange is structurally forbidden from displaying the order, and I confirmed against the ITCH 5.0 message layout that no message type names hidden interest until it executes), and has a genuinely free, self-renewing, environment-supplied label factory. It then dies anyway: G1 (three free days, ~2x10^4 independent symbol-days, Nasdaq licence not open, IEX terms unretrievable — 301 to iexcloud.io/terms, 403 on iexexchange.io) and G4 (the finance venues, not dblp, hold the prior work: 'Prediction of Hidden Liquidity in the Limit Order Book of GLOBEX Futures', Journal of Trading 2013; Hautsch & Huang 'On the hidden side of liquidity'; Bessembinder et al.; 'Hidden and Displayed Liquidity in Securities Markets with Informed Liquidity Providers' 2012 — dblp returned 0 for all three of my hidden-liquidity queries, which is the round-2 publishing-habits trap exactly).

So the round-1/2 finding needs amending. It is NOT true that F1 and F2 cannot both be satisfied — I measured a case where they both are. What is true is that the candidates which satisfy F1 and F2 die one gate later, and for a reason with structure.

A THIRD LAW, WHICH I WOULD BANK ALONGSIDE L1 AND L2:
L3 — A LABEL FACTORY BUILT ON A DELAYED OR PRIVILEGED REVELATION IS VISIBLE TO EVERYONE THE DELAY APPLIES TO. In Scout A's frame there are exactly two ways both halves of the pair can be public. (i) You simulate the destruction yourself on a public source. Then the asymmetry is your own transform, and the training regime is not the deployment regime — SuppressLine can only train on synthetic suppression in the [11,20] beneficiary band and must transfer to [1,10]; ProfileGhost's labels are, for 647k of its 691k executable functions, a property of ExeBench's input generator rather than of any recorded workload. (ii) The destruction is real and a third party reveals the truth on a schedule. Then the revelation channel is a published, well-known data product, which is precisely why the field already built the tool and why the buyer's incumbent is 'wait' or 'buy the better feed'. OfferCurve made this vivid: crossref returned four direct inverse-optimisation efforts on recovering generator offer curves from market outcomes (MISO 2015/2017, marginal offer price recovery 2023, strategic bidding 2017/2018, merit-order 2026), and market clearing is an LP so L2 applies on top. QueueGhost's buyer just subscribes to MBO. TRACECap's buyer just waits for the enhanced file. THE SAME PROPERTY THAT MAKES THE LABELS FREE AT SCALE IS WHAT MAKES G4 CROWDED AND G5 PATIENT.

Verified kills worth keeping regardless of the verdict:
- ExeBench licence is verbatim the round-2 Assemblage kill: 'All C functions keep the original license as per their original Github repository.' And its executable subset is 691,135 functions, BELOW 10^6 — the 4.5M in the abstract counts compilable, not runnable.
- CMS Medicare Provider-and-Service is public domain (data.json license = https://www.usa.gov/government-works) and has ~10^7 provider-years, but I pulled live rows and there is no suppression flag at all: suppressed rows are absent, and the by-Provider Tot_Benes is a distinct-beneficiary count that gives no arithmetic constraint. There is no manufacturer, only a synthetic proxy.
- Backmapping is not a candidate, it is a crowd: 8 direct dblp papers 2023-2026 with released models (FlowBack, HEroBM, CGBack, GenZProt, MSBack, ART-SM), 89 EuropePMC hits. Useful as proof that 'lossy, non-invertible, free labels' is necessary and nowhere near sufficient.
- Audio codec/bitrate recovery from decoded PCM, which looked like a clean physics-destroys-information case, is dead on F4 before F2: crossref returns 'Audio codec identification from coded and transcoded audios' (2013), 'COdec Recognition From Decoded Audio' (2014) and 'Audio Codec Identification with Various Encoding Configurations Using Neural Networks' (2026).
- No open limit-order-book dataset exists at target scale: HuggingFace api/datasets?search='limit order book' returned zero results.

ONE BRANCH I CLOSED FOR A NON-GATE REASON, recorded so nobody re-opens it: the de-identification inverses. The obvious Scout-A shape — recover precise locations from statutorily generalised sensitive-species records (GBIF publishes 3+ billion CC0/CC-BY occurrences, with the same specimen sometimes published precise by the institution and generalised by an aggregator, which is a genuine free label factory) — is an attack tool whose entire purpose is defeating a protection that exists to stop people locating endangered species and sensitive sites. Same for redaction-span prediction on staged declassification releases. I did not develop these, and I would not.

WHAT I DID NOT DO, so it is not credited: I measured F1 and F2 on the two ITCH candidates but measured no learning curve anywhere, so every F5 reading in this submission is an argument rather than a result. I did not read the ERCOT, FINRA or IEX terms. And I would rather report six candidates I actually attacked than pad to ten — the two extra slots past six here (SketchGhost, TRACECap) are thin, and I say so in their own fields.
```

### Round 3: scout 2 — honest read

> Scouts were told that “nothing in this structure threads it, and here is why” is a
> valid and valuable answer, and is better than padding.

```
Nothing in this structure threads the bind, and this round I can say WHY with a measurement rather than an intuition.

THE MEASUREMENT. I built the archetype of Scout B's own brief — "a SEARCH whose result is kept but whose trajectory is discarded" — as six CDCL SAT solvers (CaDiCaL 1.5.3, Glucose 4.2, Lingeling, MiniSat 2.2, MapleChrono, MergeSat 3) on random 3-SAT at n=120, m=492, and ran it on this box. Four numbers:
  (1) Determinism: re-running a solver on the same CNF returned the byte-identical model 1530/1530 = 100.00%.
  (2) Divergence: 0/339 instances had all six solvers agree; pairwise-identical rates were 0–9 of 339. So the artifact genuinely varies with the label — F1 is not the killer.
  (3) ZERO-TRAINING DECODER: "re-run all six solvers and see whose model matches" scores 0.9863 over 1,530 items, 6 classes, chance 0.1667, with only 2.55% ties.
  (4) LEARNED MODEL on 27 joint CNF-x-assignment features, held out by instance group: 0.1920 at 300 rows, 0.2031 at 900, 0.2122 at 3,000, 0.2384 at 13,500. That is +2.8 accuracy points per decade, from a 19% floor, against a free rule at 98.63%. Closing the gap at that slope needs ~27 more decades.
Scripts and data: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-raise-v1/5d675fa9-5fc4-501c-b3b3-a76510397d00/scratchpad/{sat_probe.py,rerun.py,sat_full.py,sat_learn.py,sat_data.json}.

THE LAW IT YIELDS (propose as L3, the sharpened form of L2). Let O = G_s(x): output, generator with setting s, input x. L2 said "if the generator is analytically invertible, a closed-form inverse takes the label." The SAT measurement shows inversion was never required. What is required is only that the generator be RE-RUNNABLE:

  L3. If the generator's INPUT is present in, or is a simple function of, the student's input, and the generator is executable, then re-run-and-compare is a zero-training decoder that takes the whole label. Destroying the trajectory buys nothing, because the trajectory is not needed to reproduce the output.

L3 is strictly stronger than L2 and it is retroactive: it explains AlignerPrint, CallerPrint, AssemblyProv and RefinePrint-PDB from round 2 in one sentence each, and it kills four of my eight candidates a priori — CodonPrint (protein = translate(DNA)), EngineStylo (the position is in the move list), GeomTheory (the molecular graph is read off the coordinates), SolverPrint itself.

WHY THIS IS FATAL TO SCOUT B SPECIFICALLY, NOT JUST TO MY EIGHT. Every shape in my brief — a search whose trajectory is discarded, a heuristic with tie-breaking, a many-to-one aggregation, a composed pipeline, a stochastic process whose seed is discarded, repeated requantisation — names a MACHINE generator. A machine generator is re-runnable by construction. So Scout B's shapes survive L3 only in the sub-case where the generator's input x is itself destroyed. And in that sub-case the trilemma closes:
  (a) x recoverable  -> re-run decoder, MEASURED 0.9863. Dead by A2/L3.
  (b) x destroyed, and s leaves an ARTEFACT SIGNATURE (a lattice, a block grid, a dictionary, a free-list) -> a fixed statistic reads s off. Dead by F2, with the round-1 precedents: byte-histogram nearest centroid 93.91% over 11 classes with no training loop, and the eight-line census rule at AUC 0.8297 beating the learned model everywhere below n=30,000.
  (c) x destroyed, and s leaves no trace separable from the unknown x -> the label is not a function of the input. Dead by F1, with the precedents javac 345/345 byte-identical, ELF base H=0.1518 bits, SPAdes balanced accuracy 0.503.
Only two of my eight candidates reach branch (b)/(c) at all — PresetPrint and SquashSplit — and each dies at a different gate: PresetPrint on G6 (x264 placebo at ~0.3 fps makes a 1e6 preset-labelled corpus months of 4-core wall time, and open UGC source clips number 1e3–1e5, one to three orders under the effective-N target), SquashSplit on G3 (human-legible source code, and "group these edits by intent" is what a frontier model does from a plain prompt) plus a measured G4 of at least five untangling efforts (dblp 2015 x2, 2017; Crossref 2018 program slicing, 2022 attributed graph clustering, 2025 composite-commit detection).

WHY VPT IS NOT A COUNTEREXAMPLE, AND WHERE THE REMAINING SPACE IS. VPT survives L3 because its generator is a HUMAN, and a human is not re-runnable: you cannot replay the player on the same frame and read off the keypress. The non-re-runnable generators are the ones with a person or the physical world in the loop, which is Scout A's and Scout C's territory, not mine. My honest read is that Scout B's brief, as written, selects for exactly the property that makes the label free — and that this is a structural result about the search space, not a failure of effort on these eight. I would rather this round record that finding than have me nominate PresetPrint or SquashSplit and let an adversarial review discover the same thing at greater cost. I am not putting anything forward for SELECT.
```

### Round 3: scout 3 — honest read

> Scouts were told that “nothing in this structure threads it, and here is why” is a
> valid and valuable answer, and is better than padding.

```
Nothing in this structure threads the bind, and I think I can now say WHY rather than just reporting another zero.

WHAT I MEASURED. I took the largest, cleanest, most open decision register I could find anywhere in Scout C's territory - the UK MOT test archive, OGL v3, ~500M recorded tester decisions, ~38M independent vehicles, no effective-N problem, no licence problem, no frontier-model problem, no crowding problem - and measured it end to end. I downloaded test_result_2005 (7,499,745 rows), test_result_2006 (2.78 GB raw) and test_item_2005 (9,844,490 defect rows over 1,629 RfR codes), paired 4,185,215 real 2006 tests to their 2005 prior test, and held out 400,000.

  Zero-training rule, score = age/10 + mileage/100000 .......... AUC 0.6553
  Learned HistGBM, n=1e3 / 1e4 / 1e5 / 1e6 / 3.79e6 ............ 0.5740 / 0.6349 / 0.6763 / 0.6925 / 0.6966
  Same, plus 200 prior-defect-code indicators .................. 0.5729 / 0.6371 / 0.6767 / 0.6929
  "Did it fail last year?" ..................................... AUC 0.5077
  "How many defects last year?" ................................ AUC 0.5180

Three things follow. (1) F2: the untrained two-term rule beats the learned model at every n below about 3x10^4. (2) F5: the 1.58 orders of magnitude from 1e5 to 3.8e6 bought +0.0203 AUC, and the whole prior-defect-code history - the entire richness the register contains - bought +0.0004. (3) The killer, F1: the tester's OWN PREVIOUS DECISION on the SAME VEHICLE predicts the next decision at AUC 0.5077. The channel from the published artifact to the recorded judgement is very nearly closed.

THE LAW THIS SUGGESTS, offered for the record alongside L1 and L2:

  L3. A DECISION REGISTER PUBLISHES THE DECISION, NOT THE STATE. Registers exist for accountability,
      not prediction. What they publish is a short coded row chosen so the decision can be audited and
      counted - twenty to forty fields. That row's channel capacity is fixed and small, so the learning
      curve flattens two to four orders of magnitude below 1e6 and a hand-written rule takes most of what
      is there. Where the high-dimensional state at decision time IS public, the decision labels are on
      the far side of a licence wall (eBird's terms forbid redistribution outright; OPTN needs a DUA;
      Network Rail's attribution moved to a registration-gated marketplace and the TRUST movement archive
      that would be the input has no open bulk release at all) or the field has already built and deployed
      the model (Lichess runs Irwin and Kaladin on the CC0 games; the FAA runs configuration prediction in
      TFDM; MIT published four papers on runway-configuration choice).

That is the same tension the brief describes, but sharpened: in Scout C's territory the asymmetry is not merely large, it is TOTAL. The MOT tester put the car on a ramp. The fire officer stood in the burned room. The delay-attribution clerk read the signaller's log. In every case the private information is the whole of the evidence and the public artifact is an identifier plus a date. VPT worked because consecutive video frames retain statistical evidence of the action; a registration document retains almost no statistical evidence of what a mechanic decided about a set of brake pads.

ONE CANDIDATE I COULD NOT KILL AND COULD NOT CLEAR: UK rail delay attribution. It is the only one whose artifact is genuinely high-dimensional (a graph of hundreds of trains' timings over an area and period), whose asymmetry is real without being total, whose label is a 250-way code no published rule derives from timings, and whose buyer pays nine figures a year for exactly this. I could not test it because both halves are behind walls - the labels moved to the Rail Data Marketplace and the movement-data inputs have no open historical archive. I recorded the full field list (extracted from Network Rail's own explanation PDF) so a future round can pick it up if the licence position changes, and I flagged the two fields - Responsible Manager and Incident number - that would make the study circular if left in. But UNVERIFIED is not PASS, and G1 as written demands a named licence with a URL. It does not have one.

My read: SELECT nothing. The prereg's NO VIABLE DOMAIN FOUND is the correct terminal verdict for this scout, and the MOT numbers above are the strongest single piece of evidence for it that Phase 0 has produced from this direction - a candidate that passed G1, G3, G6 and G4 cleanly and still died at F1/F2/F5 on measurement.
```

**Method.** *g4_then_adversary*: pipeline over survivors; did not run because the screen returned zero survivors → *generation*: 3 scouts aimed at the bind: (A) manufacturer's information destroyed irrecoverably in principle, (B) provably lossy non-invertible generators, (C) the label is a recorded human/institutional decision → *governed_by*: prereg/0001-phase0-terminal-verdict.json, frozen before this round ran → *screen*: 1 screener cutting L2 -> L1 -> F3 -> F2 -> F1 -> F5 -> G1/G6/G5/G3, permitted by schema to return ZERO survivors

## Round 3: white-space verification (G4) — 0 recorded searches

“I did not find anything” is not evidence of white space. Every search below was actually
run, and what came back is recorded whether or not it was convenient.

| Domain | Verdict | Crowding | Searches | Serious efforts | Open datasets |
|---|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|

## Round 3: adversarial review — 0 finalists, default posture REJECT

Each finalist was handed to an agent instructed to kill it, with the founder's previous
failure stated explicitly as the thing to look for. Several of these reviews are backed by
measurements the reviewer took on this box rather than by argument.

| Finalist | G3 | G1 | G6 | Verdict |
|---|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|

## Round 3: known limitations of this round

- Zero adversarial reviews ran in round 3 because the screen returned zero survivors. The frozen reader treats zero adversarial reviews as NO_VIABLE_DOMAIN_FOUND, and the realised path (nothing survived the screen) is a STRONGER negative than the path the preregistration's parenthetical anticipated (survivors that failed G4). Letter and substance agree; recorded because they could have disagreed.
- Scout A recorded that it measured F1 and F2 on two ITCH candidates but measured NO learning curve anywhere, so every F5 reading in its submission is an argument rather than a result. It also did not read the ERCOT, FINRA or IEX terms.
- Scout C could neither kill nor clear UK rail delay attribution: both the 250-way attribution labels (moved to the registration-gated Rail Data Marketplace) and the TRUST movement-data inputs (no open bulk historical release) are behind walls. It is recorded as UNVERIFIED, and UNVERIFIED is not PASS.
- The screener recorded one kill it could NOT sustain: PresetPrint's G4 is not crowded, so a future round must kill it on G1/G6 rather than on a G4 it does not have.
- Scout A declined to develop two candidate branches for reasons unrelated to the gates: recovering precise locations from statutorily generalised sensitive-species records, and redaction-span prediction on staged declassification releases. Both are tools whose purpose is defeating a protection. Recorded so the omission is visible rather than silent.
